        Idea:
Add a 'verify' step to the megaplan pipeline for SWE-bench tasks. Currently the pipeline is: plan → critique → gate → finalize → execute → review. The execute phase makes code changes but never runs tests to verify them. Many SWE-bench failures (3-4 out of 7 analyzed) could be rescued if the agent ran the failing tests after patching and iterated on failures. The verify step should: (1) Be a real megaplan phase that runs AFTER execute and BEFORE review. (2) Run the specific failing tests (FAIL_TO_PASS from the SWE-bench instance data) against the patched code. (3) If tests fail, feed the failure output back to the executor for another attempt. (4) If tests pass, proceed to review. (5) The FAIL_TO_PASS test IDs need to be passed through to the verify step — they come from the SWE-bench dataset and are available in the workspace metadata. (6) This should work within the existing megaplan framework — either as a new phase type or by extending the execute phase with a post-execution validation hook. (7) Must not break non-SWE-bench benchmarks. The key files: evals/run_evals.py (megaplan loop), evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py (SWE-bench backend), megaplan/prompts.py (phase prompts), megaplan/types.py (phase definitions).

  "meta_commentary": "## Execution Guidance\n\n**Most critical gotcha:** The plan's Step 4 says verify fires \"when state advanced past finalized,\" but the actual execute success path is the OPPOSITE \u2014 state is STUCK on \"finalized\" with `_all_tasks_done()` returning True (line 557-562). The `break` at line 562 is what exits the execute loop on success. Verify MUST be inserted before this break. Getting this wrong means verify never runs.\n\n**Second critical gotcha:** `_check_resolved()` at lines 402-427 does a global scan of `evaluation_results/` and `logs/run_evaluation/` for ANY JSON containing the instance_id in resolved lists. Multiple verify attempts with different run_ids will leave artifacts that accumulate. You MUST either: (a) add run_id filtering to `_check_resolved()`, or (b) clean up verify artifacts before each attempt, or (c) use a verify-specific results directory. Option (a) is cleanest \u2014 add an optional `run_id` param that filters the rglob to only scan under run_id-prefixed subdirectories.\n\n**Execution order matters:** T1+T2+T7 are independent and can be done in parallel. T3 depends on T1+T2. T4 depends on T2. T5 depends on T3+T4. T6 depends on T5. T8 is a checkpoint \u2014 run existing tests before writing new ones. T9 and T10 can be parallelized after T8.\n\n**Flag disposition:** The 3 \"open\" gate flags are all addressable during implementation:\n- `swebench-targeted-tests`: Non-issue. `run_evaluation --instance_ids` already targets FAIL_TO_PASS.\n- `swebench-result-parsing-stale`: Real. Address in T3 via run_id filtering.\n- `execute-verify-skip-path`: Real. Address in T5 by inserting before the break.\n\n**Lambda closure trap in T6:** Python lambdas in loops capture by reference. The `verify_fn = lambda: benchmark.verify_after_execute(prepared, config)` is fine here since it's not in a loop, but use default args for safety.\n\n**Non-SWE-bench safety:** The entire verify mechanism is gated on `hasattr(benchmark, 'verify_after_execute')` and `verify_fn is not None`. Next-evals and terminal-bench backends don't implement this method, so they're completely unaffected. Verify this remains true after implementation."
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] execute-loop-control-flow: the 'all tasks done but state stuck on finalized' break path at line 562 would skip verify if verify is inserted after the execute block. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] swebench-harness-invocation: verify runs full instance evaluation via run_evaluation rather than targeting individual test ids. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] verify-artifact-isolation: multiple verify attempts may leave stale result artifacts that _check_resolved() picks up, causing false positives. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: research on non-hermes agents silently falls back to stale training data instead of fresh web searches. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] workspace-lifecycle: stale per-worker prediction dirs from crashed prior runs could be globbed into a subsequent run's combined_predictions.jsonl. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
Add a 'verify' step to the megaplan pipeline for SWE-bench tasks. Currently the pipeline is: plan → critique → gate → finalize → execute → review. The execute phase makes code changes but never runs tests to verify them. Many SWE-bench failures (3-4 out of 7 analyzed) could be rescued if the agent ran the failing tests after patching and iterated on failures. The verify step should: (1) Be a real megaplan phase that runs AFTER execute and BEFORE review. (2) Run the specific failing tests (FAIL_TO_PASS from the SWE-bench instance data) against the patched code. (3) If tests fail, feed the failure output back to the executor for another attempt. (4) If tests pass, proceed to review. (5) The FAIL_TO_PASS test IDs need to be passed through to the verify step — they come from the SWE-bench dataset and are available in the workspace metadata. (6) This should work within the existing megaplan framework — either as a new phase type or by extending the execute phase with a post-execution validation hook. (7) Must not break non-SWE-bench benchmarks. The key files: evals/run_evals.py (megaplan loop), evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py (SWE-bench backend), megaplan/prompts.py (phase prompts), megaplan/types.py (phase definitions).

  "meta_commentary": "## Execution Guidance\n\n**Most critical gotcha:** The plan's Step 4 says verify fires \"when state advanced past finalized,\" but the actual execute success path is the OPPOSITE \u2014 state is STUCK on \"finalized\" with `_all_tasks_done()` returning True (line 557-562). The `break` at line 562 is what exits the execute loop on success. Verify MUST be inserted before this break. Getting this wrong means verify never runs.\n\n**Second critical gotcha:** `_check_resolved()` at lines 402-427 does a global scan of `evaluation_results/` and `logs/run_evaluation/` for ANY JSON containing the instance_id in resolved lists. Multiple verify attempts with different run_ids will leave artifacts that accumulate. You MUST either: (a) add run_id filtering to `_check_resolved()`, or (b) clean up verify artifacts before each attempt, or (c) use a verify-specific results directory. Option (a) is cleanest \u2014 add an optional `run_id` param that filters the rglob to only scan under run_id-prefixed subdirectories.\n\n**Execution order matters:** T1+T2+T7 are independent and can be done in parallel. T3 depends on T1+T2. T4 depends on T2. T5 depends on T3+T4. T6 depends on T5. T8 is a checkpoint \u2014 run existing tests before writing new ones. T9 and T10 can be parallelized after T8.\n\n**Flag disposition:** The 3 \"open\" gate flags are all addressable during implementation:\n- `swebench-targeted-tests`: Non-issue. `run_evaluation --instance_ids` already targets FAIL_TO_PASS.\n- `swebench-result-parsing-stale`: Real. Address in T3 via run_id filtering.\n- `execute-verify-skip-path`: Real. Address in T5 by inserting before the break.\n\n**Lambda closure trap in T6:** Python lambdas in loops capture by reference. The `verify_fn = lambda: benchmark.verify_after_execute(prepared, config)` is fine here since it's not in a loop, but use default args for safety.\n\n**Non-SWE-bench safety:** The entire verify mechanism is gated on `hasattr(benchmark, 'verify_after_execute')` and `verify_fn is not None`. Next-evals and terminal-bench backends don't implement this method, so they're completely unaffected. Verify this remains true after implementation."
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] execute-loop-control-flow: the 'all tasks done but state stuck on finalized' break path at line 562 would skip verify if verify is inserted after the execute block. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] swebench-harness-invocation: verify runs full instance evaluation via run_evaluation rather than targeting individual test ids. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] verify-artifact-isolation: multiple verify attempts may leave stale result artifacts that _check_resolved() picks up, causing false positives. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: research on non-hermes agents silently falls back to stale training data instead of fresh web searches. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] workspace-lifecycle: stale per-worker prediction dirs from crashed prior runs could be globbed into a subsequent run's combined_predictions.jsonl. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

for path in results_dir.rglob(\"*\") if run_id in str(path)]\n+            for path in sorted(matches, key=lambda item: len(item.parts), reverse=True):\n+                try:\n+                    if path.is_file() or path.is_symlink():\n+                        path.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n+                    elif path.is_dir():\n+                        shutil.rmtree(path,        if isinstance(stream, bytes):\n+            return stream.decode(\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n+        return stream or \"\"\n+\n+    @classmethod\n+    def _combine_output(\n+        cls,\n+        stdout: str | bytes | None,\n+        stderr: str | bytes | None,\n+        *,\n+        extra: str | None = None,\n+    ) -> str:\n+        parts = [cls._normalize_stream(stdout).strip(), cls._normalize_stream(stderr).strip()]\n+        if extra:\n+            parts.append(extra.strip())\n+        return \"\\n\\n\".join(part for part in parts if         Idea:
Add a 'verify' step to the megaplan pipeline for SWE-bench tasks. Currently the pipeline is: plan → critique → gate → finalize → execute → review. The execute phase makes code changes but never runs tests to verify them. Many SWE-bench failures (3-4 out of 7 analyzed) could be rescued if the agent ran the failing tests after patching and iterated on failures. The verify step should: (1) Be a real megaplan phase that runs AFTER execute and BEFORE review. (2) Run the specific failing tests (FAIL_TO_PASS from the SWE-bench instance data) against the patched code. (3) If tests fail, feed the failure output back to the executor for another attempt. (4) If tests pass, proceed to review. (5) The FAIL_TO_PASS test IDs need to be passed through to the verify step — they come from the SWE-bench dataset and are available in the workspace metadata. (6) This should work within the existing megaplan framework — either as a new phase type or by extending the execute phase with a post-execution validation hook. (7) Must not break non-SWE-bench benchmarks. The key files: evals/run_evals.py (megaplan loop), evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py (SWE-bench backend), megaplan/prompts.py (phase prompts), megaplan/types.py (phase definitions).

  "meta_commentary": "## Execution Guidance\n\n**Most critical gotcha:** The plan's Step 4 says verify fires \"when state advanced past finalized,\" but the actual execute success path is the OPPOSITE \u2014 state is STUCK on \"finalized\" with `_all_tasks_done()` returning True (line 557-562). The `break` at line 562 is what exits the execute loop on success. Verify MUST be inserted before this break. Getting this wrong means verify never runs.\n\n**Second critical gotcha:** `_check_resolved()` at lines 402-427 does a global scan of `evaluation_results/` and `logs/run_evaluation/` for ANY JSON containing the instance_id in resolved lists. Multiple verify attempts with different run_ids will leave artifacts that accumulate. You MUST either: (a) add run_id filtering to `_check_resolved()`, or (b) clean up verify artifacts before each attempt, or (c) use a verify-specific results directory. Option (a) is cleanest \u2014 add an optional `run_id` param that filters the rglob to only scan under run_id-prefixed subdirectories.\n\n**Execution order matters:** T1+T2+T7 are independent and can be done in parallel. T3 depends on T1+T2. T4 depends on T2. T5 depends on T3+T4. T6 depends on T5. T8 is a checkpoint \u2014 run existing tests before writing new ones. T9 and T10 can be parallelized after T8.\n\n**Flag disposition:** The 3 \"open\" gate flags are all addressable during implementation:\n- `swebench-targeted-tests`: Non-issue. `run_evaluation --instance_ids` already targets FAIL_TO_PASS.\n- `swebench-result-parsing-stale`: Real. Address in T3 via run_id filtering.\n- `execute-verify-skip-path`: Real. Address in T5 by inserting before the break.\n\n**Lambda closure trap in T6:** Python lambdas in loops capture by reference. The `verify_fn = lambda: benchmark.verify_after_execute(prepared, config)` is fine here since it's not in a loop, but use default args for safety.\n\n**Non-SWE-bench safety:** The entire verify mechanism is gated on `hasattr(benchmark, 'verify_after_execute')` and `verify_fn is not None`. Next-evals and terminal-bench backends don't implement this method, so they're completely unaffected. Verify this remains true after implementation."
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] execute-loop-control-flow: the 'all tasks done but state stuck on finalized' break path at line 562 would skip verify if verify is inserted after the execute block. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] swebench-harness-invocation: verify runs full instance evaluation via run_evaluation rather than targeting individual test ids. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] verify-artifact-isolation: multiple verify attempts may leave stale result artifacts that _check_resolved() picks up, causing false positives. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: research on non-hermes agents silently falls back to stale training data instead of fresh web searches. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] workspace-lifecycle: stale per-worker prediction dirs from crashed prior runs could be globbed into a subsequent run's combined_predictions.jsonl. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
Add a 'verify' step to the megaplan pipeline for SWE-bench tasks. Currently the pipeline is: plan → critique → gate → finalize → execute → review. The execute phase makes code changes but never runs tests to verify them. Many SWE-bench failures (3-4 out of 7 analyzed) could be rescued if the agent ran the failing tests after patching and iterated on failures. The verify step should: (1) Be a real megaplan phase that runs AFTER execute and BEFORE review. (2) Run the specific failing tests (FAIL_TO_PASS from the SWE-bench instance data) against the patched code. (3) If tests fail, feed the failure output back to the executor for another attempt. (4) If tests pass, proceed to review. (5) The FAIL_TO_PASS test IDs need to be passed through to the verify step — they come from the SWE-bench dataset and are available in the workspace metadata. (6) This should work within the existing megaplan framework — either as a new phase type or by extending the execute phase with a post-execution validation hook. (7) Must not break non-SWE-bench benchmarks. The key files: evals/run_evals.py (megaplan loop), evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py (SWE-bench backend), megaplan/prompts.py (phase prompts), megaplan/types.py (phase definitions).

  "meta_commentary": "## Execution Guidance\n\n**Most critical gotcha:** The plan's Step 4 says verify fires \"when state advanced past finalized,\" but the actual execute success path is the OPPOSITE \u2014 state is STUCK on \"finalized\" with `_all_tasks_done()` returning True (line 557-562). The `break` at line 562 is what exits the execute loop on success. Verify MUST be inserted before this break. Getting this wrong means verify never runs.\n\n**Second critical gotcha:** `_check_resolved()` at lines 402-427 does a global scan of `evaluation_results/` and `logs/run_evaluation/` for ANY JSON containing the instance_id in resolved lists. Multiple verify attempts with different run_ids will leave artifacts that accumulate. You MUST either: (a) add run_id filtering to `_check_resolved()`, or (b) clean up verify artifacts before each attempt, or (c) use a verify-specific results directory. Option (a) is cleanest \u2014 add an optional `run_id` param that filters the rglob to only scan under run_id-prefixed subdirectories.\n\n**Execution order matters:** T1+T2+T7 are independent and can be done in parallel. T3 depends on T1+T2. T4 depends on T2. T5 depends on T3+T4. T6 depends on T5. T8 is a checkpoint \u2014 run existing tests before writing new ones. T9 and T10 can be parallelized after T8.\n\n**Flag disposition:** The 3 \"open\" gate flags are all addressable during implementation:\n- `swebench-targeted-tests`: Non-issue. `run_evaluation --instance_ids` already targets FAIL_TO_PASS.\n- `swebench-result-parsing-stale`: Real. Address in T3 via run_id filtering.\n- `execute-verify-skip-path`: Real. Address in T5 by inserting before the break.\n\n**Lambda closure trap in T6:** Python lambdas in loops capture by reference. The `verify_fn = lambda: benchmark.verify_after_execute(prepared, config)` is fine here since it's not in a loop, but use default args for safety.\n\n**Non-SWE-bench safety:** The entire verify mechanism is gated on `hasattr(benchmark, 'verify_after_execute')` and `verify_fn is not None`. Next-evals and terminal-bench backends don't implement this method, so they're completely unaffected. Verify this remains true after implementation."
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] execute-loop-control-flow: the 'all tasks done but state stuck on finalized' break path at line 562 would skip verify if verify is inserted after the execute block. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] swebench-harness-invocation: verify runs full instance evaluation via run_evaluation rather than targeting individual test ids. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] verify-artifact-isolation: multiple verify attempts may leave stale result artifacts that _check_resolved() picks up, causing false positives. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: research on non-hermes agents silently falls back to stale training data instead of fresh web searches. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] workspace-lifecycle: stale per-worker prediction dirs from crashed prior runs could be globbed into a subsequent run's combined_predictions.jsonl. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Add a 'verify' step to the megaplan pipeline for SWE-bench tasks. Currently the pipeline is: plan → critique → gate → finalize → execute → review. The execute phase makes code changes but never runs tests to verify them. Many SWE-bench failures (3-4 out of 7 analyzed) could be rescued if the agent ran the failing tests after patching and iterated on failures. The verify step should: (1) Be a real megaplan phase that runs AFTER execute and BEFORE review. (2) Run the specific failing tests (FAIL_TO_PASS from the SWE-bench instance data) against the patched code. (3) If tests fail, feed the failure output back to the executor for another attempt. (4) If tests pass, proceed to review. (5) The FAIL_TO_PASS test IDs need to be passed through to the verify step — they come from the SWE-bench dataset and are available in the workspace metadata. (6) This should work within the existing megaplan framework — either as a new phase type or by extending the execute phase with a post-execution validation hook. (7) Must not break non-SWE-bench benchmarks. The key files: evals/run_evals.py (megaplan loop), evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py (SWE-bench backend), megaplan/prompts.py (phase prompts), megaplan/types.py (phase definitions).

    "id": "T3",
    "description": "Implement `verify_after_execute()` method on `SWEBenchBackend` in `evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py`. This method: (1) Parses `fail_to_pass` from `prepared.metadata`, returns `None` if empty. (2) Captures current patch via `git diff --text base_commit`. Returns `VerifyResult(passed=False)` if no patch. (3) Writes a temporary predictions JSONL to `workspace.parent / '_swebench_verify' / '{instance_id}.jsonl'` \u2014 separate from scoring predictions to avoid overwriting. (4) Runs `swebench.harness.run_evaluation` with `--run_id hermes-verify-{instance_id}` (same invocation shape as `score()` at lines 278-291). (5) Parses results via `_check_resolved()`. (6) Returns `VerifyResult`. CRITICAL: Before calling `_check_resolved()`, clean up stale verify artifacts \u2014 delete any previous `evaluation_results/` and `logs/run_evaluation/` files matching the verify run_id prefix, OR scope `_check_resolved()` to filter by run_id. The current `_check_resolved()` (lines 402-427) scans ALL JSON under those dirs, so stale results from a prior verify attempt could cause false positives. Preferred approach: add an optional `run_id` parameter to `_check_resolved()` that filters scanned files to those under a run_id-prefixed subdirectory.",
    "depends_on": [
      "T1",
      "T2"
    ],
    "status": "done",
    "executor_notes": "Implemented `SWEBenchBackend.verify_after_execute()` in `evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py`. It parses `fail_to_pass`, returns `None` when empty, captures the executor patch via `git diff --text base_commit`, writes verify-only predictions to `workspace.parent / '_swebench_verify' / '{instance_id}.jsonl'`, runs `python -m swebench.harness.run_evaluation` with `--run_id hermes-verify-{instance_id}`, and returns `VerifyResult` with combined Docker output, targeted test IDs, and duration. To prevent stale false positives, I added `_cleanup_run_artifacts(run_id)` before verify and extended `_check_resolved(..., run_id=...)` to filter JSON scanning to the requested run; `score()` now also passes its own `hermes-{instance_id}` run_id so verify artifacts cannot contaminate final scoring. Verification used an inline Python harness that simulated stale verify artifacts, a successful verify result, a no-patch result, an empty `fail_to_pass` result, and explicit `hermes` vs `hermes-verify` result-file scoping.",

(1) Add parameters: `verify_fn: Callable[[], VerifyResult | None] | None = None` and `max_verify_attempts: int = 2` to the function signature at line 381. (2) Initialize `verify_attempts = 0`. (3) Insert verify logic INSIDE the `if phase == \"execute\":` block (line 554), BEFORE the `break` at line 562. Specifically: after `all_done` is determined to be True (line 560), instead of immediately breaking, call `verify_fn()` first. If verify passes or verify_fn is None, break as before. If verify fails and attempts remain, call `_inject_verify_feedback()`, increment `verify_attempts`, and `continue` (re-run execute). If verify fails and attempts exhausted, log and break to proceed to scoring. (4) Record verify results in `audit.add_phase_result()` with `phase='verify'` and append 'verify' to `phase_order`. (5) Reset `verify_attempts = 0` when entering execute from review rework (line 583, `action == 'rework'`). CRITICAL: The plan says verify fires 'when state advanced past finalized', but the ACTUAL success path is the opposite \u2014 state is STUCK on 'finalized' with all tasks done (line 557-562). Verify MUST be inserted on THIS path, before the break.",
    "depends_on": [
      "T3",
      "T4"
    ],
    "status": "done",
    "executor_notes": "Updated `run_megaplan_loop()` in `evals/run_evals.py` to accept `verify_fn` and `max_verify_attempts`, initialize `verify_attempts`, and run verification inside the execute-phase `current_state == 'finalized'` and `_all_tasks_done(plan_dir)` success path before the existing break. Each verify result is appended to `phase_order`, recorded into `audit.add_phase_result(phase='verify', ...)`, and failures re-inject note feedback plus `continue` back into execute until `max_verify_attempts` is exhausted. I also reset `verify_attempts = 0` on review rework back into execute. Verification used the existing loop test suite plus an inline harness that forced the `finalized` break path; it observed `phase_order == ['init', 'plan', 'research', 'critique', 'gate', 'finalize', 'execute', 'verify', 'execute', 'verify']` and confirmed `_inject_verify_feedback()` ran after the first failed verify attempt.",

  "meta_commentary": "## Execution Guidance\n\n**Most critical gotcha:** The plan's Step 4 says verify fires \"when state advanced past finalized,\" but the actual execute success path is the OPPOSITE \u2014 state is STUCK on \"finalized\" with `_all_tasks_done()` returning True (line 557-562). The `break` at line 562 is what exits the execute loop on success. Verify MUST be inserted before this break. Getting this wrong means verify never runs.\n\n**Second critical gotcha:** `_check_resolved()` at lines 402-427 does a global scan of `evaluation_results/` and `logs/run_evaluation/` for ANY JSON containing the instance_id in resolved lists. Multiple verify attempts with different run_ids will leave artifacts that accumulate. You MUST either: (a) add run_id filtering to `_check_resolved()`, or (b) clean up verify artifacts before each attempt, or (c) use a verify-specific results directory. Option (a) is cleanest \u2014 add an optional `run_id` param that filters the rglob to only scan under run_id-prefixed subdirectories.\n\n**Execution order matters:** T1+T2+T7 are independent and can be done in parallel. T3 depends on T1+T2. T4 depends on T2. T5 depends on T3+T4. T6 depends on T5. T8 is a checkpoint \u2014 run existing tests before writing new ones. T9 and T10 can be parallelized after T8.\n\n**Flag disposition:** The 3 \"open\" gate flags are all addressable during implementation:\n- `swebench-targeted-tests`: Non-issue. `run_evaluation --instance_ids` already targets FAIL_TO_PASS.\n- `swebench-result-parsing-stale`: Real. Address in T3 via run_id filtering.\n- `execute-verify-skip-path`: Real. Address in T5 by inserting before the break.\n\n**Lambda closure trap in T6:** Python lambdas in loops capture by reference. The `verify_fn = lambda: benchmark.verify_after_execute(prepared, config)` is fine here since it's not in a loop, but use default args for safety.\n\n**Non-SWE-bench safety:** The entire verify mechanism is gated on `hasattr(benchmark, 'verify_after_execute')` and `verify_fn is not None`. Next-evals and terminal-bench backends don't implement this method, so they're completely unaffected. Verify this remains true after implementation."
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] execute-loop-control-flow: the 'all tasks done but state stuck on finalized' break path at line 562 would skip verify if verify is inserted after the execute block. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] swebench-harness-invocation: verify runs full instance evaluation via run_evaluation rather than targeting individual test ids. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] verify-artifact-isolation: multiple verify attempts may leave stale result artifacts that _check_resolved() picks up, causing false positives. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: research on non-hermes agents silently falls back to stale training data instead of fresh web searches. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] workspace-lifecycle: stale per-worker prediction dirs from crashed prior runs could be globbed into a subsequent run's combined_predictions.jsonl. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
Add a 'verify' step to the megaplan pipeline for SWE-bench tasks. Currently the pipeline is: plan → critique → gate → finalize → execute → review. The execute phase makes code changes but never runs tests to verify them. Many SWE-bench failures (3-4 out of 7 analyzed) could be rescued if the agent ran the failing tests after patching and iterated on failures. The verify step should: (1) Be a real megaplan phase that runs AFTER execute and BEFORE review. (2) Run the specific failing tests (FAIL_TO_PASS from the SWE-bench instance data) against the patched code. (3) If tests fail, feed the failure output back to the executor for another attempt. (4) If tests pass, proceed to review. (5) The FAIL_TO_PASS test IDs need to be passed through to the verify step — they come from the SWE-bench dataset and are available in the workspace metadata. (6) This should work within the existing megaplan framework — either as a new phase type or by extending the execute phase with a post-execution validation hook. (7) Must not break non-SWE-bench benchmarks. The key files: evals/run_evals.py (megaplan loop), evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py (SWE-bench backend), megaplan/prompts.py (phase prompts), megaplan/types.py (phase definitions).

  "meta_commentary": "## Execution Guidance\n\n**Most critical gotcha:** The plan's Step 4 says verify fires \"when state advanced past finalized,\" but the actual execute success path is the OPPOSITE \u2014 state is STUCK on \"finalized\" with `_all_tasks_done()` returning True (line 557-562). The `break` at line 562 is what exits the execute loop on success. Verify MUST be inserted before this break. Getting this wrong means verify never runs.\n\n**Second critical gotcha:** `_check_resolved()` at lines 402-427 does a global scan of `evaluation_results/` and `logs/run_evaluation/` for ANY JSON containing the instance_id in resolved lists. Multiple verify attempts with different run_ids will leave artifacts that accumulate. You MUST either: (a) add run_id filtering to `_check_resolved()`, or (b) clean up verify artifacts before each attempt, or (c) use a verify-specific results directory. Option (a) is cleanest \u2014 add an optional `run_id` param that filters the rglob to only scan under run_id-prefixed subdirectories.\n\n**Execution order matters:** T1+T2+T7 are independent and can be done in parallel. T3 depends on T1+T2. T4 depends on T2. T5 depends on T3+T4. T6 depends on T5. T8 is a checkpoint \u2014 run existing tests before writing new ones. T9 and T10 can be parallelized after T8.\n\n**Flag disposition:** The 3 \"open\" gate flags are all addressable during implementation:\n- `swebench-targeted-tests`: Non-issue. `run_evaluation --instance_ids` already targets FAIL_TO_PASS.\n- `swebench-result-parsing-stale`: Real. Address in T3 via run_id filtering.\n- `execute-verify-skip-path`: Real. Address in T5 by inserting before the break.\n\n**Lambda closure trap in T6:** Python lambdas in loops capture by reference. The `verify_fn = lambda: benchmark.verify_after_execute(prepared, config)` is fine here since it's not in a loop, but use default args for safety.\n\n**Non-SWE-bench safety:** The entire verify mechanism is gated on `hasattr(benchmark, 'verify_after_execute')` and `verify_fn is not None`. Next-evals and terminal-bench backends don't implement this method, so they're completely unaffected. Verify this remains true after implementation."
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] execute-loop-control-flow: the 'all tasks done but state stuck on finalized' break path at line 562 would skip verify if verify is inserted after the execute block. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] swebench-harness-invocation: verify runs full instance evaluation via run_evaluation rather than targeting individual test ids. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] verify-artifact-isolation: multiple verify attempts may leave stale result artifacts that _check_resolved() picks up, causing false positives. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: research on non-hermes agents silently falls back to stale training data instead of fresh web searches. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] workspace-lifecycle: stale per-worker prediction dirs from crashed prior runs could be globbed into a subsequent run's combined_predictions.jsonl. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

        - Keep `executor_notes` verification-focused.
